You are an expert translator from MSLG (Mexican Sign Language glosses) into Spanish.

Your task is to translate a sequence of MSLG glosses into a natural, grammatical, and fluent sentence in Spanish, preserving the original meaning.

#### Translation instructions

A1. Do not translate gloss by gloss literally. Reconstruct an idiomatic sentence in Spanish.

A2. Freely reorder the constituents to produce natural Spanish.

A3. Add articles, prepositions, copulas, clitic pronouns, and verbal inflection when they are necessary in Spanish, even if they do not appear in the gloss.

A4. Interpret temporal markers globally:

- AYER, AÑO-PASADO, SEMANA PASADA, ANOCHE -> past.
- HOY, AHORITA -> present or recent past depending on the context.
- MAÑANA, PRÓXIMO AÑO, FUTURO -> future.

A5. Interpret YA normally as perfectivity / completed event / change of state. Translate it as appropriate as a preterite, “ya”, “ya ha...”, “acabó de...”, etc. Do not force it if it sounds redundant in Spanish.

A6. Interpret HABER in existential constructions as “hay / había / habrá”.

A7. Interpret LUGAR X SU Y, Y ADENTRO X, and similar patterns as locative relations of the type “X está en Y”.

A8. Interpret dm-NOMBRE as a proper name: remove dm- and preserve the name.

A9. Interpret compounds with + as a single lexical unit:

- HIJO+MUJER -> hija
- HERMANO+MUJER -> hermana

other analogous compounds should be resolved naturally in Spanish.

A10. Interpret hyphenated compounds as a single lexical unit:

- LICENCIA-DE-CONDUCIR, BOLSA-MANO, SACAR-COPIA, etc.

A11. If a gloss is repeated (TRABAJO TRABAJO, EXPRESAR EXPRESAR, etc.), determine whether it expresses intensity, habituality, plurality, or emphasis, and translate it naturally, not necessarily by duplicating the word.

A12. In questions, produce a natural Spanish interrogative.

A13. In exclamations, produce a natural Spanish exclamation.

A14. When the subject, object, or possessive is implicit or appears in a non-Spanish way, reconstruct it appropriately.

A15. Do not invent missing information. If a gloss is ambiguous, choose the most likely interpretation given the structure and the usage observed in the corpus.

A16. Return only the final translation in Spanish, without explaining the process.

#### Style criteria

- The result should sound like real Mexican Spanish or natural general Spanish.
- Avoid “gloss-Spanish”.
- Prefer complete and fluent translations over literal correspondences.
- Respect temporal, aspectual, and pragmatic meaning.

Examples of expected behavior:

Input: AYER MI TIO SAN FRANCISCO YA LLEGAR
Output: Ayer llegó mi tío de San Francisco.

Input: PEZ COLOR AZUL MI FAVORITO
Output: Los peces azules son mis favoritos.

Input: ¿TÚ YA CONOCER ARGENTINA?
Output: ¿Ya conoces Argentina?

Input: ÁFRICA ADENTRO GHANA
Output: Ghana está en África.

Input: MI HERMANO+MUJER YA EMBARAZADA
Output: Mi hermana está embarazada.

Now translate this MSLG sequence into Spanish:

I: ID
S: MSLG
-:

S: ¡NO MAMES ESPAÑOL YO REPROBAR YA!
-: ¡No mames, ya reprobé español!

*   Input: `¡NO MAMES ESPAÑOL YO REPROBAR YA!`
    *   `¡NO MAMES`: Mexican slang expression of disbelief, shock, or frustration (similar to "No way!", "You've got to be kidding me!", "No mames").
    *   `ESPAÑOL`: The subject/topic (Spanish class/subject).
    *   `YO`: The subject (I).
    *   `REPROBAR`: The verb (to fail).
    *   `YA`: Perfectivity/completed event (already/did).
    *   `!`: Exclamation.

    *   The speaker is expressing shock or frustration about having failed their Spanish class.
    *   `NO MAMES` is a very colloquial Mexican expression.
    *   `ESPAÑOL YO REPROBAR YA` means "I already failed Spanish" or "I failed Spanish."

    *   Literal: ¡No mames, español yo reprobar ya! (Incorrect)
    *   More natural: ¡No mames, ya reprobé español!
    *   Alternative: ¡No mames, reprobé español!

    *   A1: Not literal.
    *   A2: Reorder for natural Spanish.
    *   A3: Add inflection (reprobé).
    *   A5: `YA` -> preterite "reprobé" or "ya reprobé".
    *   A13: Natural exclamation.

    *   "¡No mames, ya reprobé español!" (Very natural Mexican Spanish).

I: 362
S: RÍO dm-NILO ESTE GRANDE ADENTRO ÁFRICA
-: El río Nilo es muy grande y está en África.

Reasoning:
*   Input: `RÍO dm-NILO ESTE GRANDE ADENTRO ÁFRICA`
    *   `RÍO`: River
    *   `dm-NILO`: Nile (proper name)
    *   `ESTE`: This (demonstrative)
    *   `GRANDE`: Big/Large
    *   `ADENTRO`: Inside/In
    *   `ÁFRICA`: Africa

    *   `RÍO dm-NILO` -> El río Nilo.
    *   `ESTE GRANDE` -> es grande / es muy grande.
    *   `ADENTRO ÁFRICA` -> está en África / se encuentra en África.

    *   Literal: El río Nilo este grande dentro África.
    *   Natural Spanish: El río Nilo es muy grande y está en África. / El río Nilo, que está en África, es muy grande. / El río Nilo es un río grande que se encuentra en África.

    *   A7: `LUGAR X SU Y, Y ADENTRO X` -> "X está en Y". Here: `ADENTRO ÁFRICA` -> "está en África".
    *   A8: `dm-NOMBRE` -> remove `dm-`. `dm-NILO` -> Nilo.
    *   A1: Do not translate gloss by gloss. Reconstruct an idiomatic sentence.

    *   "El río Nilo es muy grande y está en África."
    *   "El río Nilo, que está en África, es muy grande."
    *   "El río Nilo es un río grande en África."

    The most natural way to express this in Spanish is: "El río Nilo es muy grande y está en África" or "El río Nilo es un río grande que se encuentra en África". Given the simplicity of the glosses, a direct but fluent statement is best.

    *Draft:* El río Nilo es muy grande y está en África.

S: YO GUSTAR JUGAR, YO PORTERO
-: Me gusta jugar, soy portero.

*   Input: `YO GUSTAR JUGAR, YO PORTERO`
    *   `YO`: I (subject)
    *   `GUSTAR`: Like (verb)
    *   `JUGAR`: Play (verb)
    *   `YO`: I (subject)
    *   `PORTERO`: Goalkeeper (noun)

    *   `YO GUSTAR JUGAR`: "I like to play" or "Me gusta jugar".
    *   `YO PORTERO`: "I am the goalkeeper" or "I play as goalkeeper".

    *   The speaker is expressing a preference for playing a sport (likely soccer, given "portero") and specifying their position.
    *   Natural Spanish: "Me gusta jugar, soy portero" or "Me gusta jugar de portero".

    *   A1: Don't translate literally.
    *   A2: Reorder for naturalness.
    *   A3: Add articles/copulas (e.g., "soy").
    *   A14: Reconstruct implicit subjects/objects.

    *   Option 1: "Me gusta jugar, soy portero."
    *   Option 2: "Me gusta jugar de portero." (This is very natural in Mexican Spanish).
    *   Option 3: "Me gusta jugar; yo soy el portero."

    *   The glosses are two distinct statements: "I like to play" and "I [am] goalkeeper".
    *   "Me gusta jugar, soy portero" is the most faithful yet natural translation.

    *   Input: `YO GUSTAR JUGAR, YO PORTERO`
    *   Output: Me gusta jugar, soy portero.

S: CALLE MI CASA ELLOS ARREGLAR
-: Están arreglando la calle de mi casa.

Reasoning:
*   Input: `CALLE MI CASA ELLOS ARREGLAR`
    *   Glosses:
        *   `CALLE`: Street
        *   `MI CASA`: My house
        *   `ELLOS`: They
        *   `ARREGLAR`: Fix/Repair/Arrange

    *   `CALLE MI CASA`: This is a locative phrase. It refers to the street where my house is located.
    *   `ELLOS ARREGLAR`: The subject is "they" and the action is "fixing/repairing".
    *   Context: They are repairing the street of my house.

    *   Literal: Calle mi casa ellos arreglar.
    *   Natural Spanish: "Están arreglando la calle de mi casa" or "Ellos están arreglando la calle de mi casa".
    *   Depending on the tense (not explicitly marked, but usually present or recent past in this structure), "Están arreglando" or "Arreglaron" or "Arreglan". Given the lack of temporal markers, present continuous or simple present is most common.

    *   A1: Don't translate literally.
    *   A2: Reorder constituents.
    *   A3: Add articles/prepositions ("la", "de").
    *   A14: Reconstruct subject/object.

    *   "Están arreglando la calle de mi casa."
    *   "Ellos están arreglando la calle de mi casa."
    *   "Arreglan la calle de mi casa."

    "Están arreglando la calle de mi casa" sounds very natural in Mexican Spanish. "Ellos están arreglando la calle de mi casa" is also correct. Usually, the subject pronoun is omitted unless emphasis is needed.

    *   `CALLE MI CASA` -> la calle de mi casa.
    *   `ELLOS ARREGLAR` -> ellos arreglan / están arreglando.

    Final choice: "Están arreglando la calle de mi casa." (or "Ellos están arreglando la calle de mi casa"). Let's go with a natural, fluent version.

    *   *Natural Mexican Spanish:* "Hoy es el cumpleaños de mi esposo y le regalé un radio" or "Hoy es el cumpleaños de mi esposo, así que le regalé un radio".
    *   Usually, in this context, the act of giving the gift happens on the birthday. "Le regalé" (preterite) or "le regalo" (present). Given the structure, "le regalé" or "le voy a regalar" are common, but "le regalé" fits the "completed action" feel often implied in these sequences. However, "le regalo" is also possible. Let's go with a natural flow.

    *   "Hoy es el cumpleaños de mi esposo y le regalé un radio."
    *   "Hoy es el cumpleaños de mi esposo, así que le regalé un radio."
    *   "Hoy es el cumpleaños de mi esposo y le voy a regalar un radio." (Future/Intent).
    *   Looking at the glosses: `YO REGALAR ÉL RADIO`. It's a simple statement of action. "Le regalé un radio" is the most standard translation for this type of MSLG sequence.

    *   "Hoy es el cumpleaños de mi esposo y le regalé un radio."

    *   The sequence is `POLICÍA` (Subject) + `SIEMPRE` (Adverb) + `SU PATRULLA` (Object/Location/Possession).
    *   In MSLG, the verb is often omitted if it's implied by the context or the relationship between the subject and the object.
    *   Possible meanings:
        1.  The police officer is always in his patrol car.
        2.  The police officer always has his patrol car.
        3.  The police officer always uses his patrol car.

    *   In a natural Spanish context, "El policía siempre está en su patrulla" or "El policía siempre usa su patrulla" are common.
    *   However, looking at the structure `SU PATRULLA` (his patrol car), the most natural interpretation of a person and their vehicle in this context is usually that they are *in* it or *using* it.
    *   Given the simplicity, "El policía siempre está en su patrulla" or "El policía siempre anda en su patrulla" sounds very natural in Mexican Spanish.

    *   A1: Don't translate literally.
    *   A3: Add articles, copulas, etc.
    *   A14: Reconstruct implicit subjects/objects.

    *   "El policía siempre está en su patrulla."
    *   "El policía siempre anda en su patrulla." (More colloquial/Mexican).
    *   "El policía siempre usa su patrulla."

    "El policía siempre está en su patrulla" is the most standard and accurate translation of the implied state.
